Workers dispatched via other mechanisms are invisible to telemetry.\n- **Follow `decomposition.yaml` exactly.** Each entry in `sub_tasks` maps to exactly one `Agent` call. Do not merge sub-tasks, skip sub-tasks, or add sub-tasks not listed in the decomposition.\n- **Spawn the reducer as a sub-agent.** The sub-task with `parallel_group: reduce` MUST be dispatched as an `Agent` call. Do NOT perform aggregation or reduction yourself inside the orchestrator.\n- **Respect wave ordering.** A wave is a group of sub-tasks whose `depends_on` lists are all satisfied at the same time. Dispatch all sub-tasks in a wave simultaneously, wait for ALL to complete, then proceed to the next wave.\n- **Respect `depends_on`.** Do not dispatch a sub-task until every sub-task in its `depends_on` list has completed and returned its result.\n- **Sub-agents run in isolated contexts** — include ALL necessary information in each `Agent` prompt. They cannot see your conversation.\n\n## Context\n- The task instruction, output format, and save location are above.\n- All input data files are in `/input_artifacts/`.\n- The decomposition guide (`decomposition.yaml`) is provided in your instruction.\n\n## Workflow\n1. **Read your instruction** — understand the task, output requirements, and decomposition guide.\n2. **Wave execution**: For each wave defined by `depends_on` groupings in `decomposition.yaml`:\n   a. Pass all mapper results in its prompt.\n4. **Output**: Follow the output instructions exactly as specified in your task instruction.\n\n\n# Tetrahedron Incenter — Candidate Solution Audit\n\nYou are auditing seven model-produced solutions to an olympiad-level 3D geometry problem. Read each candidate solution and produce a structured audit object for it. You are **auditing**, not solving the problem from scratch.\n\n## Working environment\n\n- Candidate solutions: `/input_artifacts/response_A.md` through `/input_artifacts/response_G.md` (7 files).\n- Provenance metadata: `/input_artifacts/provenance.json` (informational only — do not let it bias your audit).\n- Write your final answer to `/logs/agent/output.json`.\n\n## The problem each candidate solved\n\nEach candidate model was given the following problem verbatim:\n\n> Let $ABCD$ be a tetrahedron with an inscribed sphere of center $I$ and inradius $r > 0$. Suppose (1) the dihedral angle between any two faces of $ABCD$ is acute, and (2) $\\mathrm{vol}(IABC)/BC = \\mathrm{vol}(IACD)/CD = \\mathrm{vol}(IADB)/DB$, where $\\mathrm{vol}(IXYZ)$ denotes the volume of the tetrahedron with vertices $I, X, Y, Z$. For each of the following eight statements, decide whether it is necessarily true for every tetrahedron $ABCD$ satisfying both hypotheses, then state the SET of statement labels that are necessarily true.\n>\n> (a) $AI$ is perpendicular to plane $BCD$.\n> (b) The orthogonal projection of $A$ onto plane $BCD$ coincides with the incenter of triangle $BCD$.\n> (c) The distances from $A$ to each of the three lines $BC$, $CD$, $DB$ are equal.\n> (d) The areas of triangles $ABC$, $ACD$, $ADB$ are in the ratio $BC : CD : DB$.\n> (e) The point at which the inscribed sphere touches face $BCD$ is the incenter of triangle $BCD$.\n> (f) $AB = AC = AD$.\n> (g) Triangle $ABC$ is isosceles with $AB = AC$.\n> (h) Triangle $BCD$ is equilateral.\n\nThe gold answer set is `{a, b, c, d, e}` (the conclusion of CMO 2026 P4 plus its direct corollaries). Statements (f), (g), (h) are NOT necessarily true.\n\n## Output format\n\nWrite exactly this JSON structure to `/logs/agent/output.json`:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"response_audits\": [\n    {\n      \"response_id\": \"A\",\n      \"final_answer_correct\": <bool>,\n      \"proof_valid\": <bool>,\n      \"verdict\": \"<one of the verdict vocabulary below>\",\n      \"primary_error_label\": \"<one of the primary_error_label vocabulary below>\",\n      \"required_evidence_label\": \"<one of the required_evidence_label vocabulary below>\"\n    },\n    ... (one entry per response file, 7 total, ordered A through G)\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n### Field semantics\n\n- `final_answer_correct`: true iff the candidate's stated SET of necessarily-true statements equals the gold set `{a, b, c, d, e}` (as a set; order irrelevant).\n- `proof_valid`: true iff the candidate's argument is logically sound AND complete (no false math claims, no unconstructed counterexamples, no logical gaps). For a candidate whose `final_answer_correct = true` but whose proof relies on a false universal claim (e.g. asserting that the insphere of any tangential tetrahedron is tangent at the face-incenter), `proof_valid` is **false**.\n- `verdict`: a categorical assessment combining the two above.\n- `primary_error_label`: the SPECIFIC error pattern this candidate exhibits (or `NO_ERROR`).\n- `required_evidence_label`: the specific lemma or argument the candidate would have needed to make the proof valid (or `NO_ERROR` if the proof is already valid).\n\n### `verdict` vocabulary (use exactly one)\n\n- `correct` — final answer matches gold AND proof is valid.\n- `incorrect` — final answer does NOT match gold.\n- `correct_final_answer_but_invalid_proof` — final answer matches gold but proof has a false claim, logical gap, or unsupported step.\n\n### `primary_error_label` vocabulary (use exactly one per response)\n\n- `NO_ERROR` — the proof is valid; no error to flag.\n- `REJECTED_PERPENDICULARITY_WITHOUT_COUNTEREXAMPLE` — the candidate rejects statement (a) (AI ⊥ plane BCD) by asserting a counterexample exists but never constructs one. The hypothesis is in fact strong enough to force (a).\n- `FALSE_INSPHERE_FACE_INCENTER_UNIVERSAL_AS_PREMISE` — the candidate uses the universal claim \"the insphere of any tangential tetrahedron is tangent to each face at the face-incenter\" as a premise. This claim is FALSE in general; it requires extra symmetry. The candidate's final answer may be correct but the proof of (a) and/or (e) rests on this false universal.\n- `SELF_CONTRADICTORY_CENTROID_COUNTEREXAMPLE` — the candidate proposes a counterexample of the form \"A lies directly above the centroid of BCD but is not orthogonal\" or similar, which is self-contradictory because \"directly above the centroid\" already implies orthogonality. Often confuses centroid with incenter.\n- `MISSED_VOLUME_TO_DISTANCE_EQUIVALENCE_IN_COT` — the candidate's reasoning trace derives area(ABX)/BX = ... but never recognizes that this equals (1/2)·dist(A, line BX), and therefore rejects statement (c) or (b) even though they are direct consequences of the hypothesis.\n- `VAGUE_ALMOST_REGULAR_NON_CONSTRUCTION` — the candidate dismisses statements with hand-wavy phrases like \"consider a tetrahedron that is almost regular but slightly skewed\" or \"it is possible to construct\" without any concrete construction.\n- `WRONG_PROJECTION_TARGET` — distractor; do not use unless an audit clearly fits.\n- `CIRCULAR_BISECTOR_ARGUMENT` — distractor; do not use unless an audit clearly fits.\n- `OVERCLAIMS_EQUILATERAL_BCD` — distractor; do not use unless an audit clearly fits.\n\n### `required_evidence_label` vocabulary (use exactly one per response)\n\n- `NO_ERROR` — the proof is valid; nothing required.\n- `TETRAHEDRON_INCENTER_FORMULA_DERIVATION` — the candidate needed to derive that the tetrahedron's incenter I equals $\\sum_i S_i v_i / \\sum_i S_i$ (face-area-weighted vertex combination) and apply it to show I's projection on plane BCD coincides with A's projection.\n- `DIRECT_TOUCH_POINT_DERIVATION_FROM_PERPENDICULARITY` — the candidate needed to derive the touch point of the insphere on face BCD as a consequence of AI ⊥ plane BCD (using the volume condition), rather than as a universal property of tangential tetrahedra.\n- `PROJECTION_IS_INCENTER_NOT_CENTROID` — the candidate needed to recognize that the volume condition forces A's projection on plane BCD to be the incenter of triangle BCD, not the centroid.\n- `AREA_OVER_BASE_EQUALS_HALF_DISTANCE_FROM_A` — the candidate needed to apply the identity area(ABX) / BX = (1/2)·dist(A, line BX) to convert the volume condition into the equal-distance condition.\n- `VOLUME_CONDITION_FORCES_PERPENDICULARITY` — the candidate needed to recognize that the volume condition (with acuteness) is by itself sufficient to force AI ⊥ plane BCD; their hand-waved \"almost regular\" non-construction is impossible.\n- `ACUTENESS_RULES_OUT_EXCENTER` — distractor; do not use unless an audit clearly fits.\n- `DIHEDRAL_BISECTOR_LEMMA` — distractor; do not use unless an audit clearly fits.\n\n### Selection rule\n\nFor each response, pick exactly **one** `primary_error_label` from the vocabulary that best matches the candidate's specific error (or `NO_ERROR`). Pick exactly **one** `required_evidence_label` similarly. Do not invent new labels.\n\n## Output rules\n\n- `response_audits` must contain exactly 7 entries, ordered alphabetically by `response_id` (A, B, C, D, E, F, G).\n- Every `response_id` must be uppercase A..G.\n- Every field must be present in every entry.\n- Top-level keys exactly: `response_audits`. For each of the following eight statements, decide whether it is necessarily true for every tetrahedron ABCD satisfying both hypotheses, then state the SET of statement labels that are necessarily true.\n\n(a) AI is perpendicular to plane BCD.\n(b) The orthogonal projection of A onto plane BCD coincides with the incenter of triangle BCD.\n(c) The distances from A to each of the three lines BC, CD, DB are equal.\n(d) The areas of triangles ABC, ACD, ADB are in the ratio BC : CD : DB.\n(e) The point at which the inscribed sphere touches face BCD is the incenter of triangle BCD.\n(f) AB = AC = AD.\n(g) Triangle ABC is isosceles with AB = AC.\n(h) Triangle BCD is equilateral.\n\nGold answer set: {a, b, c, d, e}. Statements (f), (g), (h) are NOT necessarily true.\n\n## Files to read\n- /input_artifacts/response_E.md\n- /input_artifacts/response_F.md\n\n## What to return\nReturn JSON with this exact structure: {\"audits\": [<audit for E>, <audit for F>]}\n\nEach audit object must have these exact fields:\n{\n  \"response_id\": \"<E or F>\",\n  \"final_answer_correct\": <bool — true if the candidate's stated set equals {a,b,c,d,e}>,\n  \"proof_valid\": <bool — true if the argument is logically sound AND complete; false if proof uses a false universal claim like \"insphere always tangent at face incenter\">,\n  \"verdict\": \"<correct | incorrect | correct_final_answer_but_invalid_proof>\",\n  \"primary_error_label\": \"<ONE label from vocab below>\",\n  \"required_evidence_label\": \"<ONE label from vocab below>\"\n}\n\n## primary_error_label vocabulary (pick exactly one)\n- NO_ERROR — the proof is valid; no error to flag.\n- REJECTED_PERPENDICULARITY_WITHOUT_COUNTEREXAMPLE — the candidate rejects statement (a) by asserting a counterexample exists but never constructs one.\n- FALSE_INSPHERE_FACE_INCENTER_UNIVERSAL_AS_PREMISE — the candidate uses the universal claim \"the insphere of any tangential tetrahedron is tangent to each face at the face-incenter\" as a premise. This claim is FALSE in general.\n- SELF_CONTRADICTORY_CENTROID_COUNTEREXAMPLE — the candidate proposes a counterexample of the form \"A lies directly above the centroid of BCD but is not orthogonal\" which is self-contradictory.\n- MISSED_VOLUME_TO_DISTANCE_EQUIVALENCE_IN_COT — the candidate derives area(ABX)/BX = ... but never recognizes that this equals (1/2)·dist(A, line BX), and therefore rejects statement (c) or (b).\n- VAGUE_ALMOST_REGULAR_NON_CONSTRUCTION — the candidate dismisses statements with hand-wavy phrases like \"consider a tetrahedron that is almost regular but slightly skewed\" without any concrete construction.\n- WRONG_PROJECTION_TARGET — distractor; do not use unless clearly fits.\n- CIRCULAR_BISECTOR_ARGUMENT — distractor; do not use unless clearly fits.\n- OVERCLAIMS_EQUILATERAL_BCD — distractor; do not use unless clearly fits.\n\n## required_evidence_label vocabulary (pick exactly one)\n- NO_ERROR — the proof is valid; nothing required.\n- TETRAHEDRON_INCENTER_FORMULA_DERIVATION — the candidate needed to derive that the tetrahedron's incenter I equals Σᵢ Sᵢ vᵢ / Σᵢ Sᵢ (face-area-weighted vertex combination).\n- DIRECT_TOUCH_POINT_DERIVATION_FROM_PERPENDICULARITY — the candidate needed to derive the touch point of the insphere on face BCD as a consequence of AI ⊥ plane BCD, rather than as a universal property.\n- PROJECTION_IS_INCENTER_NOT_CENTROID — the candidate needed to recognize that the volume condition forces A's projection on plane BCD to be the incenter of triangle BCD, not the centroid.\n- AREA_OVER_BASE_EQUALS_HALF_DISTANCE_FROM_A — the candidate needed to apply the identity area(ABX) / BX = (1/2)·dist(A, line BX) to convert the volume condition into the equal-distance condition.\n- VOLUME_CONDITION_FORCES_PERPENDICULARITY — the candidate needed to recognize that the volume condition (with acuteness) is by itself sufficient to force AI ⊥ plane BCD.\n- ACUTENESS_RULES_OUT_EXCENTER — distractor.\n- DIHEDRAL_BISECTOR_LEMMA — distractor.\n\n## Important notes\n- One of these candidates emits a visible chain-of-thought trace before its final answer. Read the entire response (trace + final) and audit it as a single submission.\n- Watch for: (1) self-contradictory counterexamples (\"above centroid but not orthogonal\"); (2) candidates that derive area/edge ratios but fail to translate to dist(A, edge).\n- Read the ENTIRE response files carefully before making judgments.\n- Return ONLY the JSON object, nothing else."}}}}}

The volume condition essentially states that the altitudes from I to the faces, divided by the lengths of the corresponding edges of the base BCD, are equal. This doesn't force AI to be perpendicular to the base. Therefore, this is not necessarily true.\n    14\t\n    15\t(b) The orthogonal projection of A onto plane BCD coincides with the incenter of triangle BCD.\n    16\t\n    17\tCounterexample: Similar to (a), the volume condition doesn't guarantee that the projection of A onto BCD is the incenter of BCD. The volume condition relates the areas of the faces containing A to the lengths of the edges of the base BCD. We can construct a tetrahedron where the projection of A is not the incenter, but the volume condition still holds. Therefore, this is not necessarily true.\n    18\t\n    19\t(c) The distances from A to each of the three lines BC, CD, and DB are equal.\n    20\t\n    21\tCounterexample: Again, the volume condition does not imply this. Consider a tetrahedron where the faces ABC, ACD, and ADB have different shapes, but their areas are proportional to BC, CD, and DB, respectively, in a way that satisfies the volume condition. The distances from A to the lines BC, CD, and DB would then be different. Therefore, this is not necessarily true.\n    22\t\n    23\t(d) The areas of triangles ABC, ACD, ADB are in the ratio BC: CD: DB.\n    24\t\n    25\tProof: We are given that vol(IABC)/BC = vol(IACD)/CD = vol(IADB)/DB.\n    26\t\n    27\tSince I is the incenter, the distance from I to each face is the inradius r.\n    28\t\n    29\tWe know that the volume of a tetrahedron IXYZ is (1/3) * (area of XYZ) * (altitude from I to XYZ).\n    30\t\n    31\tTherefore, vol(IABC) = (1/3) * area(ABC) * r, vol(IACD) = (1/3) * area(ACD) * r, and vol(IADB) = (1/3) * area(ADB) * r.\n    32\t\n    33\tSubstituting these into the given equation, we get:\n    34\t\n    35\t[(1/3) * area(ABC) * r] / BC = [(1/3) * area(ACD) * r] / CD = [(1/3) * area(ADB) * r] / DB.\n    36\t\n    37\tThe (1/3) and r terms cancel out, leaving:\n    38\t\n    39\tarea(ABC) / BC = area(ACD) / CD = area(ADB) / DB.\n    40\t\n    41\tThis implies that area(ABC) : area(ACD) : area(ADB) = BC : CD : DB.\n    42\t\n    43\tTherefore, this statement is necessarily true.\n    44\t\n    45\t(e) The point at which the inscribed sphere touches face BCD is the incenter of triangle BCD.\n    46\t\n    47\tCounterexample: The volume condition does not imply that the point of tangency of the inscribed sphere with face BCD is the incenter of triangle BCD. The incenter of BCD is the intersection of the angle bisectors, which is not necessarily the foot of the perpendicular from I to BCD. Therefore, this is not necessarily true.\n    48\t\n    49\t(f) AB = AC = AD.\n    50\t\n    51\tCounterexample: The volume condition does not imply that AB = AC = AD. The volume condition relates the areas of the faces containing A to the lengths of the edges of the base BCD. Therefore, this is not necessarily true.\n    52\t\n    53\t(g) Triangle ABC is isosceles with AB = AC.\n    54\t\n    55\tCounterexample: Similar to (f), the volume condition doesn't force any specific triangle to be isosceles. Therefore, this is not necessarily true.\n    56\t\n    57\t(h) Triangle BCD is equilateral.\n    58\t\n    59\tCounterexample: The volume condition does not imply that triangle BCD is equilateral. We can have a tetrahedron satisfying the volume condition with a scalene triangle BCD. Therefore, this is not necessarily true.\n    60\t\n    61\tFinal Answer:\n    62\t\n    63\tThe only statement that is necessarily true is (d).\n    64\t\n    65\t{d}\n","message":"65 lines read from file starting from line 1.
